  <Package tests>
    <Dir acceptance>
      <Module test_counterfactual_experiments.py>
        Acceptance tests for ZTCF/ZVCF Counterfactual Experiments.
        
        Guideline G1 (ZTCF) and G2 (ZVCF) Implementation Tests.
        
        These tests verify that counterfactual acceleration computations work correctly
        across all physics engines that implement them.
        
        Tests:
        1. ZTCF (Zero-Torque Counterfactual): Verify drift equals ZTCF at current state
        2. ZVCF (Zero-Velocity Counterfactual): Verify gravity-dominated acceleration at v=0
        3. Causal Decomposition: Verify a_full = a_drift + a_control
        <Class TestZTCFCounterfactual>
          Tests for Zero-Torque Counterfactual (Guideline G1).
          <Function test_ztcf_equals_drift_at_current_state>
            ZTCF at current state should equal drift acceleration.
            
            When computing ZTCF at the engine's current (q, v) with \u03c4=0,
            the result should match compute_drift_acceleration().
          <Function test_ztcf_at_different_state>
            ZTCF can be computed at arbitrary (q, v), not just current state.
            
            This tests that ZTCF correctly handles state different from current.
          <Function test_ztcf_preserves_engine_state>
            Computing ZTCF should not modify the engine's internal state.
        <Class TestZVCFCounterfactual>
          Tests for Zero-Velocity Counterfactual (Guideline G2).
          <Function test_zvcf_removes_coriolis_effects>
            ZVCF should have no Coriolis/centrifugal contribution.
            
            With v=0, the Coriolis matrix C(q,v)·v = 0, so ZVCF isolates
            gravity and control effects only.
          <Function test_zvcf_at_rest_configuration>
            ZVCF at vertical (\u03b8=0) should show gravity effect.
            
            A pendulum at rest vertically should have gravitational acceleration
            trying to swing it back if perturbed, or zero if perfectly balanced.
          <Function test_zvcf_preserves_engine_state>
            Computing ZVCF should not modify the engine's internal state.
        <Class TestCausalDecomposition>
          Tests for Section F Drift-Control Superposition.
          <Function test_superposition_drift_plus_control_equals_full>
            Verify a_full = a_drift + a_control (Section F requirement).
            
            The total acceleration should decompose into drift and control components.
          <Function test_causal_attribution_ztcf>
            Verify \u0394a_control = a_full - a_ZTCF.
            
            The causal effect of control is the difference between full and ZTCF.
        <Class TestCounterfactualCrossEngine>
          Cross-engine validation for counterfactual computations.
          <Function test_ztcf_dimensionality>
            ZTCF output should have correct dimensionality (n_v,).
          <Function test_zvcf_dimensionality>
            ZVCF output should have correct dimensionality (n_v,).

    <Dir architecture>
      <Module test_dependency_direction.py>
        Architectural dependency direction tests.
        
        These tests enforce the layered architecture of the codebase by verifying
        that import dependencies flow in the correct direction:
        
            src/shared/  (lowest layer — no upward imports)
                 \u2191
            src/engines/ (may import from shared/; not from api/ or launchers/)
                 \u2191
            src/api/     (may import from engines/ and shared/; not from launchers/)
                 \u2191
            src/launchers/ (top layer — may import from any lower layer)
        
        Violations indicate architectural drift that should be corrected.
        <Class TestDependencyDirection>
          Verify that import dependencies flow in the correct architectural direction.
          <Function test_shared_does_not_import_engines>
            src/shared/ must not import from src/engines/.
          <Function test_shared_does_not_import_api>
            src/shared/ must not import from src/api/.
          <Function test_shared_does_not_import_launchers>
            src/shared/ must not import from src/launchers/.
          <Function test_engines_does_not_import_api>
            src/engines/ must not import from src/api/.
          <Function test_engines_does_not_import_launchers>
            src/engines/ must not import from src/launchers/.
          <Function test_api_does_not_import_launchers>
            src/api/ must not import from src/launchers/.
          <Function test_all_layers_summary>
            Comprehensive check — summarize all violations for visibility.

    <Package cross_engine>
      <Module test_mujoco_vs_pinocchio.py>
        Cross-engine validation tests for physics consistency.
        
        This module addresses Assessment C-006 by comparing MuJoCo physics computations
        against Pinocchio (an independent rigid body dynamics library) to verify
        scientific correctness.
        
        SCIENTIFIC RATIONALE:
        ---------------------
        Single-engine testing can hide engine-specific bugs or implementation errors.
        Cross-engine validation ensures our physics results are not artifacts of
        MuJoCo's specific algorithms but represent true physics.
        
        TEST PHILOSOPHY:
        ----------------
        1. **Simple Models**: Use analytically tractable systems (pendulum, double pendulum)
        2. **Numerical Tolerance**: Allow small differences due to numerical methods
        3. **Focus on Core Physics**: Test inverse dynamics, mass matrix, Jacobians
        4. **Graceful Degradation**: Skip if Pinocchio not installed
        
        TOLERANCE POLICY (Project Guideline P3):
        ----------------------------------------
        Cross-engine tolerances are intentionally looser than same-engine regression
        tolerances. MuJoCo and Pinocchio use different inertia conventions, geometry
        primitives, and numerical algorithms, so exact agreement is not expected.
        
        - Relative error < 0.20 (20%) for cross-engine inverse dynamics, mass matrix,
          energy, and equation-of-motion comparisons.  The pendulum models are
          constructed independently in each engine (MuJoCo from MJCF XML with
          composite geoms, Pinocchio from programmatic inertia).  Differences in
          how each engine aggregates rod + sphere inertia lead to O(10%) offsets
          that are physically plausible, not bugs.
        - Relative error < 1e-4 for Jacobian derivatives (numerical methods differ)
        - Absolute error < 1e-10 for same-engine internal consistency checks
        - Relative error < 1e-6 reserved for same-engine regression tests (not used here)
        
        REFERENCES:
        -----------
        - Carpentier et al., "Pinocchio: Fast Forward/Inverse Dynamics for Poly-Articulated Systems" (2019)
        - MuJoCo Documentation: https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/
        - Project Guidelines: docs/project_design_guidelines.qmd (Section P: Cross-Engine Validation)
        <Class TestCrossEngineInverseDynamics>
          Cross-engine validation for inverse dynamics computations.
          <Function test_simple_pendulum_zero_velocity>
            Verify inverse dynamics match for pendulum at rest.
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            For a pendulum hanging at \u03b8=0 with q\u0307=0, q\u0308=0:
            \u03c4 = g(q) = m * g * L * cos(\u03b8)
            
            Both engines should give identical gravity compensation torques.
          <Function test_simple_pendulum_with_motion>
            Verify inverse dynamics match for pendulum in motion.
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            For a swinging pendulum:
            \u03c4 = M(q)q\u0308 + C(q,q\u0307)q\u0307 + g(q)
            
            Tests both inertial and velocity-dependent terms.
        <Class TestCrossEngineMassMatrix>
          Cross-engine validation for mass matrix computations.
          <Function test_mass_matrix_consistency>
            Verify mass matrix matches between engines.
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            The mass matrix M(q) is configuration-dependent but velocity-independent.
            For a simple pendulum: M = I + m*L²
          <Function test_mass_matrix_positive_definite>
            Verify mass matrix is positive definite in both engines.
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            The mass matrix MUST be positive definite (all eigenvalues > 0).
            This is a fundamental requirement of classical mechanics.
        <Class TestCrossEngineJacobians>
          Cross-engine validation for Jacobian computations.
          <Function test_jacobian_consistency>
            Verify Jacobian matches between engines.
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            The Jacobian J maps joint velocities to Cartesian velocities:
            v = J(q) * q\u0307
            
            Both engines should produce identical Jacobians.
        <Class TestCrossEngineEnergyConsistency>
          Verify energy calculations are consistent across engines.
          <Function test_kinetic_energy_consistency>
            Verify kinetic energy calculation matches.
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            Kinetic energy: KE = 0.5 * q\u0307^T * M(q) * q\u0307
        <Class TestCrossEngineIntegration>
          Integration tests combining multiple physics computations.
          <Function test_equation_of_motion_consistency>
            Verify full equation of motion: \u03c4 = M(q)q\u0308 + C(q,q\u0307)q\u0307 + g(q).
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            This is the fundamental equation of rigid body dynamics.
            Both engines must satisfy it identically.

      <Module test_conservation_laws.py>
        Property-based tests for conservation laws.
        
        Tests fundamental physics constraints that must hold for any valid simulation:
        - Energy conservation (passive systems)
        - Momentum conservation (free-floating systems)
        - Indexed acceleration closure
        - Superposition (drift + control = full)
        
        Per Assessment B recommendations and Guideline O3/M2 requirements.
        
        Uses inline XML models to avoid external file dependencies, following
        the Self-Contained Physics Testing Pattern from Assessment B-005.
        <Class TestEnergyConservation>
          Test energy conservation in passive systems per Guideline O3.
          
          For conservative systems (no damping, no external forces), total mechanical
          energy E = KE + PE should remain constant within numerical integration error.
          
          Guideline O3 requires <1% energy drift for conservative systems.
          <Function test_pendulum_energy_conservation_mujoco>
            Test passive pendulum conserves energy (MuJoCo).
            
            Uses inline XML model (Assessment B-005 pattern).
            Initial condition: \u03b8 = 0.5 rad, \u03b8\u0307 = 0
            Duration: 5 seconds
            Tolerance: <1% energy drift (Guideline O3)
          <Function test_pendulum_energy_at_extremes>
            Test energy conservation at motion extremes.
            
            At highest point: KE \u2248 0, PE = max
            At lowest point: KE = max, PE \u2248 0
            Total should be constant.
        <Class TestIndexedAccelerationClosure>
          Test indexed acceleration closure per Guideline M2.
          
          Decomposed acceleration components (gravity, coriolis, applied, constraint)
          must sum to the total forward dynamics acceleration.
          
          Required tolerance: 1e-6 rad/s² (joint space)
          <Function test_drift_control_superposition>
            Test that drift + control = full acceleration.
            
            Section F requirement: For any state and control input,
            q\u0308_full = q\u0308_drift + q\u0308_control
            
            For MuJoCo: qacc = M^-1 * (tau + qfrc_passive - qfrc_bias)
            where qfrc_passive includes constraint forces.
            
            For a simple actuated system:
            - qacc_full = M^-1 * (tau - bias)
            - qacc_drift = M^-1 * (-bias) = acceleration with tau=0
            - qacc_control_only = M^-1 * tau
            - Superposition: qacc_full = qacc_drift + qacc_control_only
          <Function test_ztcf_equals_drift>
            Test that ZTCF (Zero-Torque Counterfactual) equals drift acceleration.
            
            Per Section G1: ZTCF isolates drift dynamics.
            a_ZTCF should equal a_drift = M^-1 * (-bias)
          <Function test_zvcf_eliminates_coriolis>
            Test that ZVCF (Zero-Velocity Counterfactual) has no velocity terms.
            
            Per Section G2: ZVCF isolates configuration-dependent dynamics.
            With v=0, Coriolis/centrifugal terms should vanish.
            
            For the pendulum test, we verify that:
            1. With v=0, acceleration depends only on gravity
            2. The acceleration matches MuJoCo's computed bias-based acceleration
        <Class TestWorkEnergyTheorem>
          Test work-energy theorem.
          
          The work done by applied forces equals the change in kinetic energy.
          W = \u222b \u03c4\xb7d\u03b8 = \u0394KE (for conservative systems with work against gravity counted)
          <Function test_work_equals_kinetic_energy_change>
            Test that applied work equals kinetic energy change.
            
            Uses the work-energy theorem: W = \u0394E_mechanical
            For a system with applied torque \u03c4: W = \u222b \u03c4\xb7d\u03b8 = \u0394KE + \u0394PE
        <Class TestConservationHelpers>
          Unit tests for energy/momentum calculation helpers.
          
          These helpers will be used by the integration tests above.
          <Function test_kinetic_energy_calculation>
            Test KE = 0.5 * qd^T * M * qd.
          <Function test_potential_energy_gravity>
            Test PE = m * g * h for simple case.
          <Function test_momentum_calculation>
            Test p = m * v for simple case.
        <Class TestNumericalPrecision>
          Test numerical precision and edge cases.
          
          Conservation laws can fail due to numerical issues:
          - Loss of precision in nearly-singular matrices
          - Accumulation of rounding errors
          - Catastrophic cancellation
          <Function test_energy_small_values>
            Test energy conservation with very small values.
          <Function test_momentum_near_zero>
            Test momentum near machine epsilon.
      <Module test_contact_cross_engine.py>
        Cross-engine contact model validation tests.
        
        Verifies contact behavior is consistent (or differences are documented) across
        MuJoCo, Drake, and Pinocchio physics engines.
        
        Contact modeling is inherently engine-specific due to different algorithms:
        - MuJoCo: Soft penalty-based contact (spring-damper)
        - Drake: Compliant + rigid contact models
        - Pinocchio: Algorithmic contact (constraint-based)
        
        This test suite:
        1. Validates basic contact physics (energy dissipation)
        2. Documents expected differences between engines
        3. Ensures no catastrophic divergence in results
        <Class TestBasicContactPhysics>

      <Module test_cross_engine_consistency.py>

      <Module test_cross_engine_validation.py>
        Cross-engine validation integration tests.
        
        Tests the CrossEngineValidator against actual physics engines to ensure
        MuJoCo, Drake, and Pinocchio produce consistent results per Guideline M2/P3.
        
        This module implements the acceptance test suite required by Section M2
        of the Project Design Guidelines.
        <Class TestCrossEngineValidator>

        <Class TestCrossEngineValidationIntegration>
          Integration tests comparing actual physics engine outputs.
          
          These tests validate Guideline M2 requirement for cross-engine comparison.
          Tests automatically skip if required engines are not available.
          <Function test_forward_dynamics_agreement>
            Validate forward dynamics agree per Guideline P3.
            
            Sets identical initial conditions and compares accelerations.
            Tolerance: acceleration ±1e-4 m/s² per P3.
          <Function test_inverse_dynamics_agreement>
            Validate inverse dynamics agree per Guideline P3.
            
            Computes torques for a given motion and compares.
            Tolerance: RMS < 10% per P3.
          <Function test_jacobian_agreement>
            Validate Jacobians agree per Guideline P3.
            
            Computes spatial Jacobians for end-effector and compares.
            Tolerance: ±1e-8 element-wise per P3.
          <Function test_ztcf_counterfactual_agreement>
            Validate ZTCF (Zero-Torque Counterfactual) agrees across engines.
            
            Per Guideline G1: ZTCF isolates drift dynamics.
          <Function test_zvcf_counterfactual_agreement>
            Validate ZVCF (Zero-Velocity Counterfactual) agrees across engines.
            
            Per Guideline G2: ZVCF isolates configuration-dependent dynamics.
          <Function test_mass_matrix_agreement>
            Validate mass matrices agree across engines.
          <Function test_indexed_acceleration_closure>
            Validate indexed acceleration closure per Guideline M2.
            
            Verifies that M(q)*qacc + bias(q,v) = tau (with tau=0 for free motion).
            The residual ||M*qacc_drift + bias|| should be near zero.
          <Function test_multi_configuration_forward_dynamics>
            Validate forward dynamics at multiple configurations per M2.
            
            Tests consistency at various (q, v) pairs spanning the
            configuration space, not just a single test point.
          <Function test_grf_cross_engine_validation>
            Validate GRF outputs agree across engines per M2/E5.
            
            For pendulum models that support contact forces, the GRF
            should be consistent across engines.

      <Module test_energy_conservation.py>
        Energy conservation tests for physics validation.
        
        This module addresses Assessment B-005 by implementing comprehensive tests
        to verify fundamental physics laws (energy conservation, work-energy theorem).
        
        These tests validate that the physics engine and analysis tools correctly
        implement Newtonian mechanics.
        <Class TestEnergyConservation>
          Test suite for energy conservation in passive systems.
          
          SCOPE (Assessment B-005):
          --------------------------
          Verifies that total mechanical energy E = KE + PE remains constant
          in conservative systems (no friction, no external forces).
          
          ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA:
          --------------------
          - Free fall: Energy drift < TOLERANCE_ENERGY_CONSERVATION (1e-6 relative)
          - Pendulum: Energy variation < TOLERANCE_ENERGY_CONSERVATION over full period
          - Zero-gravity free rotation: Angular momentum conserved
          
          PHYSICS BACKGROUND:
          -------------------
          For a conservative system: dE/dt = 0
          Equivalently: E(t) = E(0) = constant
          
          Where:
          - E = KE + PE (total mechanical energy)
          - KE = 0.5 * q\u0307^T M(q) q\u0307 (kinetic energy)
          - PE = -mgh (gravitational potential energy)
          <Function test_free_fall_energy_conservation>
            Verify energy is conserved during free fall (no damping).
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            A mass released from rest at height h should satisfy:
            - E_total = mgh (constant)
            - KE(t) + PE(t) = KE(0) + PE(0)
            - At any time: 0.5*m*v² + mgh_current = mgh_initial
            
            TEST PROCEDURE:
            ---------------
            1. Initialize pendulum at angle = 0 rad (bottom), qvel = 0
            2. Raise to height h by setting qpos = \u03c0/4 rad
            3. Simulate free fall for T seconds
            4. Verify |E(t) - E(0)| / E(0) < tolerance at all timesteps
          <Function test_work_energy_theorem>
            Verify work-energy theorem: \u0394KE = W_applied.
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            The work-energy theorem states:
            \u0394KE = \u222b F \xb7 ds = \u222b \u03c4 \xb7 q\u0307 dt
            
            For actuated motion, the change in kinetic energy should equal
            the work done by applied torques.
            
            TEST PROCEDURE:
            ---------------
            1. Start pendulum at rest (\u03b8=0, q\u0307=0)
            2. Apply constant torque \u03c4 for time T
            3. Measure:
               - \u0394KE = KE_final - KE_initial
               - W = \u222b \u03c4 \xb7 q\u0307 dt (numerical integration)
            4. Verify |\u0394KE - W| / |W| < TOLERANCE_WORK_ENERGY_MISMATCH (5%)
          <Function test_power_balance>
            Verify instantaneous power balance: dE/dt = P_in - P_out.
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            The instantaneous power balance states:
            dE/dt = P_actuator - P_dissipation
            
            For a conservative system (no damping):
            dE/dt = \u03c4 \xb7 q\u0307 (actuator power)
            
            TEST PROCEDURE:
            ---------------
            1. Compute dE/dt numerically from energy time series
            2. Compute P_actuator = \u03c4 \xb7 q\u0307 at each timestep
            3. Verify dE/dt \u2248 P_actuator (within numerical differentiation error)
        <Class TestConservationLaws>
          Additional conservation law tests (momentum, angular momentum).
          <Function test_zero_gravity_angular_momentum_conservation>
            Verify angular momentum conservation in zero gravity free rotation.
            
            PHYSICS:
            --------
            In the absence of external torques:
            dL/dt = 0 (angular momentum conserved)
            
            Where L = I\xb7\u03c9 (for rigid body) or L = \u03a3 I_i\xb7\u03c9_i (for multi-body system)
            
            This test verifies that MuJoCo correctly implements angular momentum
            conservation for free rotation.

      <Module test_mujoco_protocol.py>
        <UnitTestCase TestMuJoCoProtocol>
          A class whose instances are single test cases.
          
          By default, the test code itself should be placed in a method named
          'runTest'.
          
          If the fixture may be used for many test cases, create as
          many test methods as are needed. When instantiating such a TestCase
          subclass, specify in the constructor arguments the name of the test method
          that the instance is to execute.
          
          Test authors should subclass TestCase for their own tests. Construction
          and deconstruction of the test's environment ('fixture') can be
          implemented by overriding the 'setUp' and 'tearDown' methods respectively.
          
          If it is necessary to override the __init__ method, the base class
          __init__ method must always be called. It is important that subclasses
          should not change the signature of their __init__ method, since instances
          of the classes are instantiated automatically by parts of the framework
          in order to be run.
          
          When subclassing TestCase, you can set these attributes:
          * failureException: determines which exception will be raised when
              the instance's assertion methods fail; test methods raising this
              exception will be deemed to have 'failed' rather than 'errored'.
          * longMessage: determines whether long messages (including repr of
              objects used in assert methods) will be printed on failure in *addition*
              to any explicit message passed.
          * maxDiff: sets the maximum length of a diff in failure messages
              by assert methods using difflib. It is looked up as an instance
              attribute so can be configured by individual tests if required.
          <TestCaseFunction test_set_control_size_check>
          <TestCaseFunction test_set_state_calls_forward>

      <Module test_physics_consistency.py>
        Cross-engine physics consistency tests (Issue #126).
        
        Verifies that physics engines produce consistent results for identical
        initial conditions. Uses the PendulumPhysicsEngine as a baseline that
        runs without external dependencies, and compares MuJoCo/Drake/Pinocchio
        when available.
        
        Guideline references:
            M2 - Cross-engine deterministic validation
            P3 - Tolerance-based comparison
        <Class TestPendulumAnalytical>
          Validate PendulumPhysicsEngine against analytical properties.
          <Function test_mass_matrix_symmetric_positive_definite>
            Mass matrix M(q) must be symmetric and positive-definite.
          <Function test_mass_matrix_varies_with_configuration>
            Mass matrix should change with joint angles (coupled inertia).
          <Function test_gravity_forces_zero_at_vertical>
            At q=0 (hanging straight down), gravity torques should be zero.
          <Function test_gravity_forces_nonzero_at_angle>
            At non-zero angle, gravity torques should be non-zero.
          <Function test_drift_acceleration_matches_forward_dynamics>
            Drift acceleration should equal -M^{-1} * bias at zero velocity.
          <Function test_inverse_dynamics_consistency>
            tau = M*qacc + bias should be self-consistent.
          <Function test_inverse_dynamics_unit_acceleration>
            Inverse dynamics with known qacc should give M*qacc + bias.
          <Function test_stepping_changes_state>
            Stepping the engine should change the state (non-equilibrium).
          <Function test_checkpoint_restore_consistency>
            Save and restore checkpoint should preserve state exactly.
          <Function test_deterministic_stepping>
            Same initial conditions should produce identical trajectories.
        <Class TestCrossEngineConsistency>
          Cross-engine consistency checks using real physics engines.
          
          These tests require MuJoCo, Drake, and/or Pinocchio to be installed.
          They verify that different engines produce consistent results for the
          same initial conditions on the same model.
          <Function test_pendulum_consistency_mujoco_drake>
            Verify simple pendulum dynamics match between MuJoCo and Drake.
          <Function test_pendulum_consistency_mujoco_pinocchio>
            Verify simple pendulum dynamics match between MuJoCo and Pinocchio.
          <Function test_pendulum_consistency_drake_pinocchio>
            Verify simple pendulum dynamics match between Drake and Pinocchio.
          <Function test_all_engines_mass_matrix_spd>
            Mass matrix from every available engine must be SPD.
          <Function test_all_engines_inverse_dynamics_consistency>
            tau = M*qacc + bias must hold across all engines.

      <Module test_process_isolation_strict.py>

    <Module test_circular_dependencies.py>
      Tests verifying no circular dependencies between top-level modules.
      
      Phase 1 of the decoupling plan eliminates circular imports between:
      - shared <-> engines  (capabilities moved to shared, loaders moved to engines)
      - shared <-> robotics (control_interface uses lazy import)
      - api -> launchers    (extracted to api.services.launcher_service)
      - deployment -> engines/learning (TYPE_CHECKING guards, lazy imports)
      
      These tests verify that key modules can be imported independently without
      pulling in modules they should not depend on.

    <Module test_dashboard_enhancements.py>
      Tests for dashboard enhancements.
      <UnitTestCase TestDashboardEnhancements>
        A class whose instances are single test cases.
        
        By default, the test code itself should be placed in a method named
        'runTest'.
        
        If the fixture may be used for many test cases, create as
        many test methods as are needed. When instantiating such a TestCase
        subclass, specify in the constructor arguments the name of the test method
        that the instance is to execute.
        
        Test authors should subclass TestCase for their own tests. Construction
        and deconstruction of the test's environment ('fixture') can be
        implemented by overriding the 'setUp' and 'tearDown' methods respectively.
        
        If it is necessary to override the __init__ method, the base class
        __init__ method must always be called. It is important that subclasses
        should not change the signature of their __init__ method, since instances
        of the classes are instantiated automatically by parts of the framework
        in order to be run.
        
        When subclassing TestCase, you can set these attributes:
        * failureException: determines which exception will be raised when
            the instance's assertion methods fail; test methods raising this
            exception will be deemed to have 'failed' rather than 'errored'.
        * longMessage: determines whether long messages (including repr of
            objects used in assert methods) will be printed on failure in *addition*
            to any explicit message passed.
        * maxDiff: sets the maximum length of a diff in failure messages
            by assert methods using difflib. It is looked up as an instance
            attribute so can be configured by individual tests if required.
        <TestCaseFunction test_live_plot_ground_forces>
          Test plotting Ground Forces.
        <TestCaseFunction test_live_plot_widget_modes>
          Test LivePlotWidget new modes.
        <TestCaseFunction test_unified_window_static_plots>
          Test new static plot options in UnifiedDashboardWindow.

          <Class TestValidateModelPathContract>
            Design by Contract tests for validate_model_path function.
            
            Preconditions:
            - model_path must be a string representing a relative path
            - model_path must not contain '..' (parent directory references)
            - model_path must not be an absolute path
            
            Postconditions:
            - Returns a string path that exists within allowed directories
            - Raises HTTPException with 400 for invalid paths
            - Raises HTTPException with 404 for non-existent files
            <Function test_returns_string>
              Postcondition: Returns a string when path is valid.

        <Module test_checkpoint.py>
          Tests for the checkpoint save/restore functionality.
          <UnitTestCase TestStateCheckpoint>
            A class whose instances are single test cases.
            
            By default, the test code itself should be placed in a method named
            'runTest'.
            
            If the fixture may be used for many test cases, create as
            many test methods as are needed. When instantiating such a TestCase
            subclass, specify in the constructor arguments the name of the test method
            that the instance is to execute.
            
            Test authors should subclass TestCase for their own tests. Construction
            and deconstruction of the test's environment ('fixture') can be
            implemented by overriding the 'setUp' and 'tearDown' methods respectively.
            
            If it is necessary to override the __init__ method, the base class
            __init__ method must always be called. It is important that subclasses
            should not change the signature of their __init__ method, since instances
            of the classes are instantiated automatically by parts of the framework
            in order to be run.
            
            When subclassing TestCase, you can set these attributes:
            * failureException: determines which exception will be raised when
                the instance's assertion methods fail; test methods raising this
                exception will be deemed to have 'failed' rather than 'errored'.
            * longMessage: determines whether long messages (including repr of
                objects used in assert methods) will be printed on failure in *addition*
                to any explicit message passed.
            * maxDiff: sets the maximum length of a diff in failure messages
                by assert methods using difflib. It is looked up as an instance
                attribute so can be configured by individual tests if required.
            <TestCaseFunction test_creation>

            <Function test_linear_signal_small_jerk>
              Linear signal: jerk depends on differentiation method.
              
              compute_jerk uses Savitzky-Golay filter with polyorder=2,
              so for a true linear signal the derivative should be approx constant.
              The magnitude depends on slope * fs scaling.

        <Class TestStepResponse>
          Test step response behavior.
          <Function test_activation_rise_time>
            Test activation rise time with step input 0 -> 1.
            
            For first-order system with varying time constant, we expect
            approximately 63% response at \u03c4 and 95% at ~3\u03c4.
            
            Note: time constant increases with activation (\u03c4 = \u03c4_act * (0.5 + 1.5*a)),
            so convergence is slower than simple first-order system.
          <Function test_deactivation_fall_time>
            Test deactivation fall time with step input 1 -> 0.
            
            Deactivation should be slower than activation (40ms vs 10ms).
          <Function test_activation_faster_than_deactivation>
            Test that activation is faster than deactivation.
          <Function test_step_response_monotonic_increase>
            Test that activation increases monotonically with constant u > a.
          <Function test_step_response_monotonic_decrease>
            Test that activation decreases monotonically with constant u < a.
        <Class TestPhysiologicalRealism>
          Test physiological realism of the model.
          <Function test_typical_time_constants>
            Test that typical time constants produce physiologically realistic responses.
            
            Based on literature (Thelen 2003):
            - \u03c4_act \u2248 10 ms (fast calcium release)
            - \u03c4_deact \u2248 40 ms (slow calcium pump)
          <Function test_asymmetric_response>
            Test asymmetric activation/deactivation is physiologically realistic.
            
            Activation (calcium release) is faster than deactivation (calcium pump).
            This is a key physiological characteristic of muscle dynamics.
          <Function test_minimum_activation_prevents_division_by_zero>

      <Module test_aerodynamics.py>
        Unit tests for aerodynamics module.
        
        TDD tests covering:
        - AerodynamicsConfig: Toggle on/off, tunable parameters
        - WindModel: Random wind, gusts, direction changes
        - EnvironmentRandomizer: Stochastic environment simulation
        - AerodynamicsEngine: Unified force calculations with toggle support
        
        Following Pragmatic Programmer principles:
        - Reversible: All effects can be toggled
        - Reusable: Modular, composable components
        - DRY: No duplication in test setup
        - Orthogonal: Independent test cases

      <Module test_assessment_scripts.py>
        <UnitTestCase TestAssessmentScripts>
          A class whose instances are single test cases.
          
          By default, the test code itself should be placed in a method named
          'runTest'.
          
          If the fixture may be used for many test cases, create as
          many test methods as are needed. When instantiating such a TestCase
          subclass, specify in the constructor arguments the name of the test method
          that the instance is to execute.
          
          Test authors should subclass TestCase for their own tests. Construction
          and deconstruction of the test's environment ('fixture') can be
          implemented by overriding the 'setUp' and 'tearDown' methods respectively.
          
          If it is necessary to override the __init__ method, the base class
          __init__ method must always be called. It is important that subclasses
          should not change the signature of their __init__ method, since instances
          of the classes are instantiated automatically by parts of the framework
          in order to be run.
          
          When subclassing TestCase, you can set these attributes:
          * failureException: determines which exception will be raised when
              the instance's assertion methods fail; test methods raising this
              exception will be deemed to have 'failed' rather than 'errored'.
          * longMessage: determines whether long messages (including repr of
              objects used in assert methods) will be printed on failure in *addition*
              to any explicit message passed.
          * maxDiff: sets the maximum length of a diff in failure messages
              by assert methods using difflib. It is looked up as an instance
              attribute so can be configured by individual tests if required.
          <TestCaseFunction test_assess_J_logic>
          <TestCaseFunction test_extract_score_from_report>

          - Driver: 163 mph ball speed, 11\xb0 launch, 2500 rpm \u2192 ~275 yards carry

            Test driver carry distance is in reasonable range.
            
            The current empirical model targets:
            - Driver (73 m/s, 11°, 2500 rpm): ~200 yards

            Wider tolerance allows for different coefficient sets.

        <Class TestSpinDecay>
          Tests for spin decay model in BallFlightSimulator.
          <Function test_default_spin_decay_rate>
            Test BallProperties has default spin decay rate from physics constants.
          <Function test_custom_spin_decay_rate>
            Test custom spin decay rate can be set.
          <Function test_zero_spin_decay_rate>
            Test spin decay can be disabled by setting rate to zero.
          <Function test_spin_decay_reduces_carry>
            Spin decay should reduce carry distance vs constant spin.
            
            With spin decaying, Magnus lift decreases over time, leading
            to a shorter carry than if spin were constant.
          <Function test_higher_decay_rate_less_carry>
            Higher spin decay rate should produce shorter carry.
          <Function test_spin_decay_exponential_rate>
            Verify spin decays approximately 20-30% over 4 seconds at default rate.
            
            With decay_rate=0.05, after 4s: spin(4) = spin(0) * exp(-0.05 * 4)
            = spin(0) * exp(-0.2) \u2248 spin(0) * 0.819 \u2192 ~18% decay
          <Function test_no_spin_no_decay_effect>
            With zero initial spin, decay rate should not affect trajectory.

      <Module test_drake_wrapper.py>
        <UnitTestCase TestDrakeWrapper>
          A class whose instances are single test cases.
          
          By default, the test code itself should be placed in a method named
          'runTest'.
          
          If the fixture may be used for many test cases, create as
          many test methods as are needed. When instantiating such a TestCase
          subclass, specify in the constructor arguments the name of the test method
          that the instance is to execute.
          
          Test authors should subclass TestCase for their own tests. Construction
          and deconstruction of the test's environment ('fixture') can be
          implemented by overriding the 'setUp' and 'tearDown' methods respectively.
          
          If it is necessary to override the __init__ method, the base class
          __init__ method must always be called. It is important that subclasses
          should not change the signature of their __init__ method, since instances
          of the classes are instantiated automatically by parts of the framework
          in order to be run.
          
          When subclassing TestCase, you can set these attributes:
          * failureException: determines which exception will be raised when
              the instance's assertion methods fail; test methods raising this
              exception will be deemed to have 'failed' rather than 'errored'.
          * longMessage: determines whether long messages (including repr of
              objects used in assert methods) will be printed on failure in *addition*
              to any explicit message passed.
          * maxDiff: sets the maximum length of a diff in failure messages
              by assert methods using difflib. It is looked up as an instance
              attribute so can be configured by individual tests if required.
          <TestCaseFunction test_forward_computation>
            Test forward() triggers computation of derived quantities.
          <TestCaseFunction test_forward_with_no_context>
            Test forward() raises PreconditionError when context is missing.
          <TestCaseFunction test_reset_logic>
            Test that reset() properly resets state to defaults.
          <TestCaseFunction test_step_caching>
            Test that Simulator is cached and reused in step().

      <Module test_muscle_contribution_closure.py>
        Tests for muscle contribution closure in induced acceleration analysis.
        
        Scientific Background:
            Induced Acceleration Analysis decomposes total acceleration into contributions:
                a_total = \u03a3 a_muscle_i + a_passive + a_external
        
            This test verifies the fundamental closure property: the sum of all muscle-induced
            accelerations should equal the total acceleration (when no external torques applied).
        
        References:
            - Zajac, F. E. (2002). "Understanding muscle coordination of the human leg with
              dynamical simulations." Journal of Biomechanics.
            - Anderson, F. C., & Pandy, M. G. (2003). "Individual muscle contributions to
              support in normal walking." Gait & Posture.
        
        Refactored to use shared engine availability module (DRY principle).
        <Class TestMuscleContributionClosure>
          <Function test_skipped_no_myosuite>
        <Class TestMuscleContributionComplexModels>
          <Function test_skipped_no_myosuite>

      <Module test_property_based.py>
        Property-based tests for core mathematical operations.
        
        Uses hypothesis to verify mathematical invariants hold for arbitrary inputs.
        <Class TestRotationMatrixProperties>
          Property-based tests for rotation matrices.
          <Function test_rotation_matrix_is_orthogonal>
            Rotation matrices should be orthogonal: R @ R.T = I.
          <Function test_rotation_matrix_determinant_is_one>
            Rotation matrices should have determinant = 1.
          <Function test_euler_roundtrip>
            Converting euler->matrix->euler should preserve the rotation (avoiding gimbal lock).
          <Function test_rotation_preserves_vector_norm>
            Rotating a vector should preserve its length.
        <Class TestSkewMatrixProperties>
          Property-based tests for skew-symmetric matrices.
          <Function test_skew_is_antisymmetric>
            Skew matrix should be antisymmetric: S = -S.T.
          <Function test_skew_diagonal_is_zero>
            Skew matrix diagonal should be all zeros.
          <Function test_skew_cross_product_equivalence>
            skew(v) @ u should equal cross(v, u).
        <Class TestNumericalStability>
          Property-based tests for numerical stability.
          <Function test_mean_within_bounds>
            Mean of values should be within min/max bounds (up to floating-point rounding).
          <Function test_positive_sum_is_positive>
            Sum of positive values should be positive.
      <Module test_property_based_physics.py>
        Property-based tests for physics, dynamics, and planning modules.
        
        Uses the hypothesis library to verify invariants and mathematical properties
        that must hold across a wide range of randomly generated inputs.
        <Class TestBallFlightProperties>
          Property-based tests for BallFlightSimulator force calculations.
          <Function test_gravity_always_pulls_downward>
            Property: gravity force z-component is always negative.
          <Function test_drag_force_opposes_velocity>
            Property: drag force opposes the velocity direction.
            
            The dot product of drag force and relative velocity should be
            non-positive (drag decelerates the ball).
          <Function test_magnus_force_perpendicular_to_spin_and_velocity>
            Property: Magnus force is perpendicular to the spin axis and velocity.
            
            The Magnus force is proportional to (spin_axis x velocity_hat),
            so it should be approximately perpendicular to the spin axis.
          <Function test_zero_wind_zero_spin_drag_antiparallel_to_velocity>
            Property: with zero wind and zero spin, drag is anti-parallel to velocity.
          <Function test_forces_scale_with_air_density>
            Property: aerodynamic forces scale linearly with air density.
        <Class TestPhaseDetectionProperties>
          Property-based tests for PhaseDetectionMixin.
          <Function test_detected_phases_cover_entire_time_range>
            Property: detected phases cover the full time range (no gaps).
          <Function test_phase_boundaries_monotonically_increasing>
            Property: phase start times are monotonically non-decreasing.
          <Function test_each_phase_has_nonnegative_duration>
            Property: every detected phase has non-negative duration.
        <Class TestSwingOptimizerConfigProperties>
          Property-based tests for SwingOptimizer configuration invariants.
          <Function test_bounds_lower_leq_upper>
            Property: optimization bounds always have lower <= upper.
          <Function test_initial_guess_within_bounds>
            Property: initial guess angle variables are within joint-limit bounds.
            
            The velocity portion of the initial guess is computed via np.gradient
            and may slightly exceed the hard velocity bounds; we verify that
            the *angle* portion (first half of the vector) respects the
            flexibility-scaled joint-limit bounds, and that the velocity portion
            stays within a generous 10 rad/s tolerance of its bounds.
        <Class TestPhysicalConstantsProperties>
          Property-based tests for physical constants validity.
          <Function test_gravity_within_expected_range>
            Property: gravity is within the expected range (9.7 to 9.9 m/s^2).
          <Function test_all_physical_constants_positive_and_finite>
            Property: all physical constants defined via PhysicalConstant are positive and finite.
          <Function test_precomputed_floats_match_source_constants>
            Property: pre-computed float values match their source constants.
          <Function test_derived_constants_consistent>
            Property: derived constants are consistent with base values.
          <Function test_conversion_factor_roundtrip>
            Property: unit conversion roundtrips preserve value.
        <Class TestRRTPlannerProperties>
          Property-based tests for RRT motion planner.
          <Function test_path_start_equals_requested_start>
            Property: planned path starts at the requested start point.
          <Function test_path_end_within_goal_tolerance>
            Property: planned path end is within goal tolerance of the goal.
          <Function test_consecutive_points_within_step_size>
            Property: consecutive path points are within step_size of each other.
